Uh, yeah… I guess I’m here because something’s been off with my eating lately. For the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite at all, which isn’t really like me. I work security, and after a shift I’d usually eat something I actually like, you know, something easy or kind of comforting, but lately I’ll sit there with it in front of me and it just… doesn’t appeal to me. I end up forcing down a few bites more because I know I should eat than because I want to.

It’s gotten to the point where I noticed I’m kind of dreading meals, or I’ll make food and then just let it sit there. I don’t know, it feels strange to come in just saying “I’m not hungry,” but it’s been going on enough that I figured I should talk to someone about it.

I haven’t really noticed my clothes fitting different, not in any clear way. Like my belt or my work pants aren’t suddenly hanging off me or anything that obvious. I think the bigger thing I’ve noticed is just how little I’m eating compared to normal.

Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking about the appetite itself exactly, but I have been feeling pretty down. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.

I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.

What I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.

The thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.

I don’t know, it feels strange to come in just saying “I’m not hungry,” but it’s been going on enough that I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not actually sure exactly how much, because I haven’t been checking the scale closely. I wouldn’t want to say a number and be wrong. What I’ve noticed more is just that for the last few weeks I’ve barely been eating like I normally do. Like after a shift, I’ll have something I usually like sitting there, and it just kind of sits in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I probably should, but it’s not because I’m hungry.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve lost some, I don’t know. It feels possible, just from how little I’ve been getting down, but I haven’t really measured it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my clothes fitting different, not in any clear way. Like my belt or my work pants aren’t suddenly hanging off me or anything that obvious. I think the bigger thing I’ve noticed is just how little I’m eating compared to normal.\n\nFor the last few weeks, I’ll get off a shift and have something I’d usually look forward to, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should, but it’s more like I’m making myself do it, not because I actually want it. So maybe if it keeps going I’d notice the clothes thing more, but right now I can’t honestly say I’ve seen that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your lack of appetite or anything else lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking about the appetite itself exactly, but I have been feeling pretty down. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.\n\nEven when I’m home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people around, I’m just… not really there, if that makes sense. I’ll be physically sitting there, hearing people talk, but inside I feel kind of distant and weighed down. And I think the eating thing is tied up in that somehow, because food just feels like another thing I’m supposed to do rather than something I actually want.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a significant amount, because I haven’t really been tracking my weight or stepping on a scale much. I came in more because the appetite change itself has been going on for the last few weeks, and it’s noticeable for me.\n\nLike, after a shift I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually be ready to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, not like I’m actually hungry. So if I’ve lost weight, I couldn’t give you a real number. The main thing is just that I’m barely eating compared to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. I work security, and after a shift I’d usually eat something I actually like, you know, something easy or kind of comforting, but lately I’ll sit there with it in front of me and it just… doesn’t appeal to me. I don’t know, it feels strange to come in just saying “I’m not hungry,” but it’s been going on enough that I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not actually sure exactly how much, because I haven’t been checking the scale closely. I wouldn’t want to say a number and be wrong. What I’ve noticed more is just that for the last few weeks I’ve barely been eating like I normally do. Like after a shift, I’ll have something I usually like sitting there, and it just kind of sits in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I probably should, but it’s not because I’m hungry.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve lost some, I don’t know. It feels possible, just from how little I’ve been getting down, but I haven’t really measured it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my clothes fitting different, not in any clear way. Like my belt or my work pants aren’t suddenly hanging off me or anything that obvious. I think the bigger thing I’ve noticed is just how little I’m eating compared to normal.\n\nFor the last few weeks, I’ll get off a shift and have something I’d usually look forward to, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should, but it’s more like I’m making myself do it, not because I actually want it. So maybe if it keeps going I’d notice the clothes thing more, but right now I can’t honestly say I’ve seen that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your lack of appetite or anything else lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking about the appetite itself exactly, but I have been feeling pretty down. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.\n\nEven when I’m home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people around, I’m just… not really there, if that makes sense. I’ll be physically sitting there, hearing people talk, but inside I feel kind of distant and weighed down. And I think the eating thing is tied up in that somehow, because food just feels like another thing I’m supposed to do rather than something I actually want.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a significant amount, because I haven’t really been tracking my weight or stepping on a scale much. I came in more because the appetite change itself has been going on for the last few weeks, and it’s noticeable for me.\n\nLike, after a shift I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually be ready to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, not like I’m actually hungry. So if I’ve lost weight, I couldn’t give you a real number. The main thing is just that I’m barely eating compared to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. If anything, the thing that’s been bothering me is that I’m just not hungry.\n\nLike after work, I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of look at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.

But I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. That’s honestly what got my attention more than the weight itself. I’ll get home after a shift, and I’ll have food there that I normally like — the kind of thing I usually wouldn’t have any problem eating — and it just sits there. I’ll look at it for a while, maybe take a couple bites because I know I should eat something, but then I’m done. It feels forced, like I’m eating because I’m supposed to, not because I actually want it.

So, noticeable weight loss? I can’t really say for sure. But the appetite change has been noticeable, definitely.

It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.\n\nEven when I’m home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people around, I’m just… not really there, if that makes sense. I’ll be physically sitting there, hearing people talk, but inside I feel kind of distant and weighed down. And I think the eating thing is tied up in that somehow, because food just feels like another thing I’m supposed to do rather than something I actually want.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a significant amount, because I haven’t really been tracking my weight or stepping on a scale much. I came in more because the appetite change itself has been going on for the last few weeks, and it’s noticeable for me.\n\nLike, after a shift I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually be ready to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, not like I’m actually hungry. So if I’ve lost weight, I couldn’t give you a real number. The main thing is just that I’m barely eating compared to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. That’s really not where my head is with it.

It’s more that I just haven’t had much of an appetite for the last few weeks. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I’m not eating less to avoid gaining weight. It’s not like I’m looking at food and thinking about calories or trying to control my weight or anything like that.

It’s more like… for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite at all. I’ll get home after work, and there’ll be something there I usually like — something I’d normally be glad to eat after a shift — and I just don’t want it. It’ll sit there in front of me, and I’ll kind of pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I’m hungry. It feels more like I’m just going through the motions. So no, it’s not about gaining weight. I just haven’t been able to eat like I normally do.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, yeah… I guess I’m here because something’s been off with my eating lately. For the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite at all, which isn’t really like me. I work security, and after a shift I’d usually eat something I actually like, you know, something easy or kind of comforting, but lately I’ll sit there with it in front of me and it just… doesn’t appeal to me. I end up forcing down a few bites more because I know I should eat than because I want to.\n\nIt’s gotten to the point where I noticed I’m kind of dreading meals, or I’ll make food and then just let it sit there. I don’t know, it feels strange to come in just saying “I’m not hungry,” but it’s been going on enough that I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not actually sure exactly how much, because I haven’t been checking the scale closely. I wouldn’t want to say a number and be wrong. What I’ve noticed more is just that for the last few weeks I’ve barely been eating like I normally do. Like after a shift, I’ll have something I usually like sitting there, and it just kind of sits in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I probably should, but it’s not because I’m hungry.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve lost some, I don’t know. It feels possible, just from how little I’ve been getting down, but I haven’t really measured it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my clothes fitting different, not in any clear way. Like my belt or my work pants aren’t suddenly hanging off me or anything that obvious. I think the bigger thing I’ve noticed is just how little I’m eating compared to normal.\n\nFor the last few weeks, I’ll get off a shift and have something I’d usually look forward to, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should, but it’s more like I’m making myself do it, not because I actually want it. So maybe if it keeps going I’d notice the clothes thing more, but right now I can’t honestly say I’ve seen that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your lack of appetite or anything else lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking about the appetite itself exactly, but I have been feeling pretty down. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.\n\nEven when I’m home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people around, I’m just… not really there, if that makes sense. I’ll be physically sitting there, hearing people talk, but inside I feel kind of distant and weighed down. And I think the eating thing is tied up in that somehow, because food just feels like another thing I’m supposed to do rather than something I actually want.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a significant amount, because I haven’t really been tracking my weight or stepping on a scale much. I came in more because the appetite change itself has been going on for the last few weeks, and it’s noticeable for me.\n\nLike, after a shift I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually be ready to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, not like I’m actually hungry. So if I’ve lost weight, I couldn’t give you a real number. The main thing is just that I’m barely eating compared to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. If anything, the thing that’s been bothering me is that I’m just not hungry.\n\nLike after work, I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of look at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. I don’t know if it’s been a significant change on a scale.\n\nWhat I do know is that my eating has been way off for the last few weeks. That’s really why I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and have something there I normally like, something I’d usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but I’m not hungry, and it feels like I’m forcing it down. So maybe my weight has changed, but I haven’t checked.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.\n\nBut I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. That’s honestly what got my attention more than the weight itself. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. Yeah… I think it probably is connected, at least it feels that way to me. I mean, I can’t prove that’s what’s causing it or anything, but the timing lines up. These last few weeks, when I’ve been feeling low, that’s when my appetite has really dropped off.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n17\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Uh, yeah… I guess I’m here because something’s been off with my eating lately. For the last few weeks I’ve barely had any appetite at all, which isn’t really like me. I work security, and after a shift I’d usually eat something I actually like, you know, something easy or kind of comforting, but lately I’ll sit there with it in front of me and it just… doesn’t appeal to me. I end up forcing down a few bites more because I know I should eat than because I want to.\n\nIt’s gotten to the point where I noticed I’m kind of dreading meals, or I’ll make food and then just let it sit there. I don’t know, it feels strange to come in just saying “I’m not hungry,” but it’s been going on enough that I figured I should talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not actually sure exactly how much, because I haven’t been checking the scale closely. I wouldn’t want to say a number and be wrong. What I’ve noticed more is just that for the last few weeks I’ve barely been eating like I normally do. Like after a shift, I’ll have something I usually like sitting there, and it just kind of sits in front of me. I’ll take a few bites because I know I probably should, but it’s not because I’m hungry.\n\nSo… maybe I’ve lost some, I don’t know. It feels possible, just from how little I’ve been getting down, but I haven’t really measured it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed your clothes fitting differently since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t really noticed my clothes fitting different, not in any clear way. Like my belt or my work pants aren’t suddenly hanging off me or anything that obvious. I think the bigger thing I’ve noticed is just how little I’m eating compared to normal.\n\nFor the last few weeks, I’ll get off a shift and have something I’d usually look forward to, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll take a few bites because I know I should, but it’s more like I’m making myself do it, not because I actually want it. So maybe if it keeps going I’d notice the clothes thing more, but right now I can’t honestly say I’ve seen that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or depressed about your lack of appetite or anything else lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I wouldn’t say I’m panicking about the appetite itself exactly, but I have been feeling pretty down. It’s hard to explain without sounding dramatic. Most days it feels like I’m carrying around this heavy, flat feeling, like everything is kind of muted.\n\nEven when I’m home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people around, I’m just… not really there, if that makes sense. I’ll be physically sitting there, hearing people talk, but inside I feel kind of distant and weighed down. And I think the eating thing is tied up in that somehow, because food just feels like another thing I’m supposed to do rather than something I actually want.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a significant amount, because I haven’t really been tracking my weight or stepping on a scale much. I came in more because the appetite change itself has been going on for the last few weeks, and it’s noticeable for me.\n\nLike, after a shift I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually be ready to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it feels forced, not like I’m actually hungry. So if I’ve lost weight, I couldn’t give you a real number. The main thing is just that I’m barely eating compared to normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. If anything, the thing that’s been bothering me is that I’m just not hungry.\n\nLike after work, I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of look at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. I don’t know if it’s been a significant change on a scale.\n\nWhat I do know is that my eating has been way off for the last few weeks. That’s really why I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and have something there I normally like, something I’d usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but I’m not hungry, and it feels like I’m forcing it down. So maybe my weight has changed, but I haven’t checked.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.\n\nBut I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. That’s honestly what got my attention more than the weight itself. I’ll get home after a shift, and I’ll have food there that I normally like — the kind of thing I usually wouldn’t have any problem eating — and it just sits there. I’ll look at it for a while, maybe take a couple bites because I know I should eat something, but then I’m done. It feels forced, like I’m eating because I’m supposed to, not because I actually want it.\n\nSo, noticeable weight loss? I can’t really say for sure. But the appetite change has been noticeable, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. That’s really not where my head is with it.\n\nIt’s more that I just haven’t had much of an appetite for the last few weeks. Like I’ll come home after a shift and there’ll be food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of stare at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat something, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. It’s not me trying not to eat because of weight or anything like that. I’m just… not hungry.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating less because you're trying to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not eating less to avoid gaining weight. It’s not like I’m looking at food and thinking about calories or trying to control my weight or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite at all. I’ll get home after work, and there’ll be something there I usually like — something I’d normally be glad to eat after a shift — and I just don’t want it. It’ll sit there in front of me, and I’ll kind of pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I’m hungry. It feels more like I’m just going through the motions. So no, it’s not about gaining weight. I just haven’t been able to eat like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of appetite is related to your feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is connected, at least it feels that way to me. I mean, I can’t prove that’s what’s causing it or anything, but the timing lines up. These last few weeks, when I’ve been feeling low, that’s when my appetite has really dropped off.\n\nIt’s not like food tastes bad exactly, and it’s not like I’m trying not to eat. It’s more like I get home after a shift and there’s something there I usually like, something I’d normally be happy to eat, and I just don’t have any interest in it. I’ll kind of sit there looking at it, maybe take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels forced. Like my body just isn’t asking for it.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I do think it’s tied together with feeling down. The lack of appetite was actually one of the things that made me think something wasn’t right, because it’s been going on for weeks and it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite started around the same time as your feelings of being down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it seemed to start around the same time. I came in because, for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite, and that’s been happening right alongside me feeling down.\n\nIt’s been strange because after a shift, normally I’d come home and eat something without really thinking about it, especially if it was food I usually like. But lately it just sits there. I’ll look at it and know I probably should eat, so I force down a few bites, but there’s no real hunger behind it. So… yeah, the appetite thing and the low mood feel like they kind of showed up together.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think so, yeah… I mean, I can’t know for sure, but that’s how it feels. Before I started feeling this way, I didn’t really have to think about eating. After work I’d come home, and if there was something I liked, I’d just eat it. It wasn’t this whole thing where I had to talk myself into it.

Lately, for the last few weeks, it’s been like I’ll have food in front of me — even stuff I normally like after a shift — and it just sits there. I’ll stare at it, maybe move it around a little, take a few bites because I know I probably should, but there’s not much appetite there. It feels forced.

So… yeah, if I wasn’t feeling so down, I think I probably would have more of an appetite. At least I don’t remember it being like this before.

I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. I’ll get home after a shift and have something there I normally like, something I’d usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but I’m not hungry, and it feels like I’m forcing it down. Not every single minute in a loud obvious way, but most days there’s this heavy, flat feeling that kind of follows me around.\n\nLike I can be home with my family, or sitting in the break room at work with people talking around me, and I’m there, but I don’t really feel there. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, but I feel weighed down and kind of distant from everything. It takes up a lot of the day, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly haven’t been weighing myself much, so I can’t give you a number or say for sure how much has changed. The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. If anything, the thing that’s been bothering me is that I’m just not hungry.\n\nLike after work, I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of look at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. I don’t know if it’s been a significant change on a scale.\n\nWhat I do know is that my eating has been way off for the last few weeks. That’s really why I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and have something there I normally like, something I’d usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but I’m not hungry, and it feels like I’m forcing it down. So maybe my weight has changed, but I haven’t checked.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.\n\nBut I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. That’s honestly what got my attention more than the weight itself. I’ll get home after a shift, and I’ll have food there that I normally like — the kind of thing I usually wouldn’t have any problem eating — and it just sits there. I’ll look at it for a while, maybe take a couple bites because I know I should eat something, but then I’m done. Like I’ll come home after a shift and there’ll be food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of stare at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat something, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. It’s not me trying not to eat because of weight or anything like that. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. If anything, the thing that’s been bothering me is that I’m just not hungry.\n\nLike after work, I’ll have food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of look at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. I don’t know if it’s been a significant change on a scale.\n\nWhat I do know is that my eating has been way off for the last few weeks. That’s really why I came in. I’ll get home after a shift and have something there I normally like, something I’d usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but I’m not hungry, and it feels like I’m forcing it down. So maybe my weight has changed, but I haven’t checked.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.\n\nBut I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. That’s honestly what got my attention more than the weight itself. I’ll get home after a shift, and I’ll have food there that I normally like — the kind of thing I usually wouldn’t have any problem eating — and it just sits there. I’ll look at it for a while, maybe take a couple bites because I know I should eat something, but then I’m done. It feels forced, like I’m eating because I’m supposed to, not because I actually want it.\n\nSo, noticeable weight loss? I can’t really say for sure. But the appetite change has been noticeable, definitely.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. That’s really not where my head is with it.\n\nIt’s more that I just haven’t had much of an appetite for the last few weeks. Like I’ll come home after a shift and there’ll be food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of stare at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat something, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. It’s not me trying not to eat because of weight or anything like that. I’m just… not hungry.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating less because you're trying to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not eating less to avoid gaining weight. It’s not like I’m looking at food and thinking about calories or trying to control my weight or anything like that.\n\nIt’s more like… for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite at all. I’ll get home after work, and there’ll be something there I usually like — something I’d normally be glad to eat after a shift — and I just don’t want it. It’ll sit there in front of me, and I’ll kind of pick at it. I’ll force down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I’m hungry. It feels more like I’m just going through the motions. So no, it’s not about gaining weight. I just haven’t been able to eat like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of appetite is related to your feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is connected, at least it feels that way to me. I mean, I can’t prove that’s what’s causing it or anything, but the timing lines up. These last few weeks, when I’ve been feeling low, that’s when my appetite has really dropped off.\n\nIt’s not like food tastes bad exactly, and it’s not like I’m trying not to eat. It’s more like I get home after a shift and there’s something there I usually like, something I’d normally be happy to eat, and I just don’t have any interest in it. I’ll kind of sit there looking at it, maybe take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels forced. Like my body just isn’t asking for it.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I do think it’s tied together with feeling down. The lack of appetite was actually one of the things that made me think something wasn’t right, because it’s been going on for weeks and it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite started around the same time as your feelings of being down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it seemed to start around the same time. I came in because, for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite, and that’s been happening right alongside me feeling down.\n\nIt’s been strange because after a shift, normally I’d come home and eat something without really thinking about it, especially if it was food I usually like. But lately it just sits there. I’ll look at it and know I probably should eat, so I force down a few bites, but there’s no real hunger behind it. So… yeah, the appetite thing and the low mood feel like they kind of showed up together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an appetite if you weren't feeling down?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… I mean, I can’t know for sure, but that’s how it feels. Before I started feeling this way, I didn’t really have to think about eating. After work I’d come home, and if there was something I liked, I’d just eat it. It wasn’t this whole thing where I had to talk myself into it.\n\nLately, for the last few weeks, it’s been like I’ll have food in front of me — even stuff I normally like after a shift — and it just sits there. I’ll stare at it, maybe move it around a little, take a few bites because I know I probably should, but there’s not much appetite there. Yeah… I think the down feeling was there first, or at least that’s what I noticed first. It’s hard to separate them exactly because they sort of blended together after a while, but the main thing I remember is this heavy, flat feeling most days.

The reason I came in is more that, for the last few weeks, my appetite has been really off.\n\nLike after a shift, I’ll have food in front of me that I normally like, and usually I’d be ready to eat, but now it just kind of sits there. I end up forcing down a few bites because I know I should eat something, but it doesn’t feel like I actually want it. So if my weight has changed, I’m not sure exactly how much, but I’m definitely eating way less than normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I really don’t know about the 5% part. I haven’t been weighing myself, so I don’t want to say yes to a number I can’t actually back up.\n\nWhat I can say is that for the last few weeks my appetite has been way off. After work, I’ll have something there that I normally like and would usually eat without thinking, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll make myself take a few bites because I know I should eat, but it feels forced. So I might have lost weight, but I can’t say it’s more than 5%. The appetite change is what I’ve really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in how your body looks, such as loss of muscle mass or fat, since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure about my body looking different, to be honest. I haven’t stood in front of the mirror and thought, like, “Oh, I’ve lost muscle,” or “I look thinner.” I don’t know that I’d notice right away anyway.\n\nThe thing I have noticed is more just how little I’ve been eating. For the last few weeks, I barely have an appetite. I’ll come home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually be happy to eat, and it just kind of sits there. I’ll end up making myself take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels like I’m forcing it. So maybe that could be changing how I look, but I can’t really say I’ve clearly noticed fat or muscle loss.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any loose-fitting clothes that used to fit you well?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve specifically noticed my clothes hanging off me or anything like that. Like, I haven’t put on a pair of pants and thought, “Wow, these are loose now.” I’m not really checking that closely, I guess.\n\nWhat I did notice, and what made me come in, is that for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. I’ll get home after a shift and have food there that I normally like, something I’d usually eat no problem, and it just sits there. I’ll kind of stare at it, take a couple bites because I know I should eat, and then I’m done. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I haven’t really picked up on it yet, but I know I’m eating a lot less than I usually do.\n\nDoctor: Are you worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I’m worried about gaining weight. That’s not really what’s going on for me. It sits there, and I know I should eat, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. That’s more why I came in — it’s been a few weeks of that, and it’s not normal for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight?\n\nPatient: I haven’t actually weighed myself, so I can’t say a number or anything like that. So maybe my weight has changed, but I haven’t checked.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a noticeable amount of weight since your appetite changed?\n\nPatient: I haven’t noticed it in a way where I can say, like, “Yeah, I’ve lost this much,” because I still haven’t weighed myself or anything. I don’t really keep track of that.\n\nBut I wouldn’t be surprised if I have lost some, just because of how little I’ve been eating these last few weeks. It feels forced, like I’m eating because I’m supposed to, not because I actually want it.\n\nSo, noticeable weight loss? I can’t really say for sure. Like I’ll come home after a shift and there’ll be food there that I normally like, stuff I’d usually eat without even thinking about it, and I’ll just kind of stare at it. It sits there, and I know I should eat something, so I’ll make myself take a few bites, but it feels forced. It’s not me trying not to eat because of weight or anything like that. I’m just… not hungry.\n\nDoctor: Are you eating less because you're trying to avoid gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not eating less to avoid gaining weight. It feels more like I’m just going through the motions. So no, it’s not about gaining weight. I just haven’t been able to eat like I normally do.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your lack of appetite is related to your feeling down or depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it probably is connected, at least it feels that way to me. These last few weeks, when I’ve been feeling low, that’s when my appetite has really dropped off.\n\nIt’s not like food tastes bad exactly, and it’s not like I’m trying not to eat. It’s more like I get home after a shift and there’s something there I usually like, something I’d normally be happy to eat, and I just don’t have any interest in it. I’ll kind of sit there looking at it, maybe take a few bites because I know I should, but it feels forced. Like my body just isn’t asking for it.\n\nSo yeah, I guess I do think it’s tied together with feeling down. The lack of appetite was actually one of the things that made me think something wasn’t right, because it’s been going on for weeks and it’s not really like me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your lack of appetite started around the same time as your feelings of being down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it seemed to start around the same time. I came in because, for the last few weeks, I’ve barely had an appetite, and that’s been happening right alongside me feeling down.\n\nIt’s been strange because after a shift, normally I’d come home and eat something without really thinking about it, especially if it was food I usually like. But lately it just sits there. I’ll look at it and know I probably should eat, so I force down a few bites, but there’s no real hunger behind it. So… yeah, the appetite thing and the low mood feel like they kind of showed up together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would have an appetite if you weren't feeling down?\n\nPatient: I think so, yeah… I mean, I can’t know for sure, but that’s how it feels. Before I started feeling this way, I didn’t really have to think about eating. After work I’d come home, and if there was something I liked, I’d just eat it. It wasn’t this whole thing where I had to talk myself into it.\n\nLately, for the last few weeks, it’s been like I’ll have food in front of me — even stuff I normally like after a shift — and it just sits there. I’ll stare at it, maybe move it around a little, take a few bites because I know I probably should, but there’s not much appetite there. So if my clothes are fitting differently, I guess I haven’t paid close attention, but the appetite part has definitely changed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your depression started before your lack of appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think the down feeling was there first, or at least that’s what I noticed first. It’s like I’m carrying this weight around with me, and everything feels kind of muted. Then the appetite seemed to drop off along with that. So I’d say the low mood came first, or at least it was the thing that stood out before I really noticed I wasn’t eating much."
